# E2E Workflow Suite Implementation Plan: every workflow walked on the dev database, desktop and phone, light and dark, and the fixes it finds

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** the E2E workflow suite of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-e2e-workflow-suite-design.md` (approved by the user on 2026-10-19), on `feat/e2e-suite` at `fd74d004` (the spec's commit on `master` `db3f796f`). After it:
- **the suite is in the repo** (`e2e/`, `playwright.config.ts`, `npm run test:e2e`, out of `npm test`): Playwright 1.61.1 (the browser cache's version: nothing is downloaded) and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0; a guard that stops on anything but `asset_management_dev` / `asset_dev` before the app starts; a setup that creates the run's department, room, employees, four `E2E-` roles with exactly spec §7's permissions and four users through the app; four projects (desktop and phone, light and dark); every test measured (console errors, failed requests, axe WCAG 2.1 AA serious and critical, load times, screenshots, one summary file) (Tasks 1–5, the foundation);
- **every workflow of spec §6 is walked with real writes** as the person the spec names: asset transactions, the user's first worry (Tasks 6–12); the field audit walk and its review, the user's second worry, the phone on WebKit (Tasks 13–17); repair records and the PM plan (Tasks 18–21); disposal with its segregation of duties and the open-round block (Tasks 22–24); reports and exports, roles and permissions, errors under the field once per workflow form (Tasks 25–27);
- **the app findings are fixed** (spec §9, the user's "แก้ไขให้เลย"): each with its own E2E step as the red test, a source or unit test, the fix, and the green run (Tasks 29–44); the six that need a decision are asked first and fixed on the answer (Tasks 45–50);
- **the controller runs the whole suite in all four projects and fixes until it is green** (Tasks 28, 51), **reports to the user in Thai** with the human UAT checklist of spec §3.5 (Task 52), **records it in the docs** (Task 53), and merges only with the user (Task 54).

No schema change without the user (Task 45 asks), no write to `asset_management`, no new dependency beyond the two the spec approved, no deploy.

**Architecture:**
- **One branch, one chain, in this order:** the foundation (Tasks 1–5, ids EF1–EF5), then the five areas as rehearsed (TX1–TX7, EA1–EA5, ER1–ER4, ED1–ED3, EM1–EM3, Tasks 6–27), then the controller's first whole run (Task 28), one suite fix (SF1, Task 29), the fifteen app fixes (FX1–FX15, Tasks 30–44), the six decisions (DX1–DX6, Tasks 45–50), the controller's green run (Task 51), the report (52), the docs (53) and the final task (54). Each implementer task is one commit with its unit test; the tree is green after every commit.
- **The chapters keep their drafts' text.** The foundation and each area were drafted and rehearsed by their own agent (records below); their tasks are reproduced here as rehearsed, renumbered, with the assembly's rulings (AS-R1–AS-R14) on top. Inside a chapter, an area's own words "Task n" mean its area-local number; the chapter's heading table maps each to its global number.
- **Task ids** in test names and commit messages are the drafts' (`EF1`… `TX1`… `EA1`… `ER1`… `ED1`… `EM1`…), and `SF1`, `FX1`–`FX15`, `DX1`–`DX6` for the fix round. Rulings: `EF-R*` (foundation), `TX-R*`, `EA-R*`, `ER-R*`, `ED-R*`, `EM-R*` (areas), `AS-R*` (this assembly). Findings: `A1` (foundation), `TX-A*` (transactions), `AU*`/`N-AU*` (audit), `AR*` (repair), `D*` (disposal), `M*` (cross-cutting); decisions for the user `U1`, `U3`.
- **Unit test files** (new, LF; `npm test` sweeps them): `tests/e2e-suite.test.ts` (EF1–EF5), `tests/e2e-transactions.test.ts` (TX1–TX7), `tests/e2e-audit.test.ts` (EA1–EA5), `tests/e2e-repair.test.ts` (ER1–ER4), `tests/e2e-disposal.test.ts` (ED1–ED3), `tests/e2e-cross-cutting.test.ts` (EM1–EM3), `tests/e2e-fixes.test.ts` (SF1 creates it; FX1–FX15 and DX1–DX6 append), `tests/e2e-docs.test.ts` (Task 53). The E2E specs themselves (`e2e/**/*.spec.ts`) are the tests of each Step 2 and Step 5 that changes what the suite does in a browser.
- **Controller tasks:** Task 0 (branch, baseline, `.env` check, `.env.e2e`, browsers, the one build of the app as it is), Task 28 (the whole suite before the fixes: the red list), Task 51 (the whole suite in all projects until green), Task 52 (the report), Task 54 (verify, wiki, memory, merge only with the user). Task 53 (docs) is an implementer task that stops if Task 51's findings file is missing. Every E2E run is a controller step; implementers run `node --test`, `npm test`, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` only.

**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16.4 App Router (production build, `next start`) · React 19 · Prisma 7 + SQL Server (`asset_management_dev`; no schema change but by Task 45 with the user) · next-intl 4 (Thai-primary) · `node --test` with Node 24 type stripping (strip-only: no parameter properties, no enums in modules the tests import) · `@playwright/test` 1.61.1 · `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 (axe-core 4.13.0) · ExcelJS 4 (already a dependency) to open the exports.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-e2e-workflow-suite-design.md` (approved). Coverage: §5 and §7–§8 (Tasks 0–5), §6.1 (Tasks 6–12), §6.2 (Tasks 13–17), §6.3 (Tasks 18–21), §6.4 (Tasks 22–24), §6.5–§6.7 (Tasks 25–27; §6.7 also inside every area), §9 (Tasks 28–51), §3.4 and §3.5 (Task 52), §11 calls (the rulings named in "Spec coverage" at the end). Left to the builder (§10) and decided here: the file layout, the helpers, the setup through the app, the run id, the split into tests, the allow-list entries (four, each reviewed), the run order (one worker, the areas' order), one shared setup per run.

## Global Constraints

- **Git:**
  - Work in the main checkout `D:/Antigravity/asset-system` on `feat/e2e-suite` (it holds `fd74d004`). Never run `git checkout`, `git switch`, `git reset`, `git stash`, `git rebase` or `git clean`; never touch `master` but in Task 54 with the user; never push.
  - Commit only the files your task names: `git add <paths>` (quote paths with brackets or parentheses), never `git add -A` or `git add .`.
  - The checkout has untracked items that are not yours: `.codex/`, `.impeccable/config.json`, `.impeccable/critique/`, `.playwright-mcp/`, `.superpowers/brainstorm/`, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-22-audit-component-assets.untracked-backup.md`; another session may add `.claude/`. Leave them alone.
  - End every commit message with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` (each Step 6 passes it as a second `-m`).
- **Line endings** (`git ls-files --eol`; `core.autocrlf` is `true`):
  - Every new text file is LF, written with a file tool, never a shell heredoc or `node -e` (the drafts lost backslashes through both). The two fixtures are binary (`i/-text w/-text`).
  - An existing file keeps what `git ls-files --eol` shows: `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `eslint.config.mjs` `i/lf w/lf`; `.gitignore` `i/lf w/mixed`; the app files the fix round edits are `w/crlf` but `src/components/assets/asset-qr-code.tsx`, `src/lib/uploads.ts`, `src/lib/asset-operation-document.ts` and the two print pages under `src/app/[locale]/(print)/asset-management/{checkouts,transfers}/[id]/page.tsx` (`w/lf`). Edit with exact-string replacement (the Edit tool, or `node <atools>/edit.mjs`), never a formatter, never `sed -i`.
  - **Never write a test that depends on working-tree line endings** (AS-R4): every unit test that reads a file strips `\r`. In Git Bash `grep -c $'\r'` reports 0 even for CRLF files: check with `node <tools>/eol.mjs <file>…` (an LF file prints 0 and its line count; a CRLF file the same number twice).
  - `git add` of an LF working-tree file prints "LF will be replaced by CRLF the next time Git touches it": expected; do not renormalize.
- **The database and the hazards** (spec §5, §11; the user's decision: `asset_management_dev`):
  - The suite writes to `asset_management_dev` through the app only. No direct SQL write; a `SELECT` read is fine. No cleanup: every record carries the run id.
  - Never: LDAP sync or test, the settings page's save (`PUT /api/admin/settings`), Excel import, a failed login, HR/SMF/HIP, deleting data, printing a password or a connection string. `asset_management` is never touched; `.env.prod-admin` is never opened.
  - **No failed login** (spec §11): a local password that fails is passed to LDAP (`src/lib/auth.ts:107–128`) and the dev settings point at the company AD. Every sign-in is `signInOnce` (EF2) with a known account; nothing retries a sign-in (`retries: 0`). A sign-in that times out on the database stops the run before any password check (`authenticateCredentials` throws at its first `prisma.user.findUnique`, DN2): find out why, never retry in code.
  - Never open, read or print `.env`, `.env.prod-admin` or `.env.e2e`. The controller writes `.env.e2e` (Task 0); implementers never do.
- **Running the suite** (controller):
  - A production build of the tree under test: `rm -rf .next/cache/turbopack`, then `npm run build`. The app code changes only in Tasks 29–50: one build serves Tasks 1–28; Tasks 30–50 and 51 build again after their source change (each says so).
  - Then `E2E_PORT=<port> npm run test:e2e` or `E2E_PORT=<port> npx playwright test <specs>`. The suite starts `next start -p <port>` itself and stops it (`reuseExistingServer: false`: a server already on the port stops the run). The controller's port is 3100 unless taken (`netstat -ano | grep LISTENING | grep ":<port> "` prints nothing); the drafts' ports 3121–3127 were the rehearsal's.
  - **One suite run at a time** on the database (AS-R10): parallel runs can take the same run id (TX-S1, N-AU4, SN1, DN3, N12) and load the shared SQL Server. In Git Bash set `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` for a command whose variable looks like a path.
  - Every invocation creates one run (the setup project: a department, a room, six employees, four roles, four users) plus what its specs write; the next run takes the next letter.
  - After every run: `node <tools>/scan-secrets.mjs` prints 0 hits; nothing listens on the port.

### What each chapter adds to these constraints

The chapters' own "Global Constraints" sections hold, with this list on top; where they differ, this section wins (AS-R1).
- **Transactions (Tasks 6–12):** `e2e/support/allow-list.ts` is the area's one shared edit (TX-R9); every measured page goes through `visit`, `record` and `visitAssetPage` of `e2e/transactions/tx-support.ts` (TX-R5, TX-R6), but `findings.spec.ts`, which must see the known findings.
- **Audit (Tasks 13–17):** EA3 edits `e2e/support/measure-core.ts` and `e2e/support/measure.ts` (the offline window, EA-R5); every task also runs `node --test tests/e2e-suite.test.ts`. One serial chain per project (EA-R1).
- **Repair (Tasks 18–21):** no file outside `e2e/repair/` and its unit test. The PM list has no search: ER4 opens it with `?pageSize=100` (SN4).
- **Disposal (Tasks 22–24):** no file outside `e2e/disposal/` and its unit test. A full run writes per project 3 rooms, 5 assets, 5 requests, 1 batch, 2 evidence PDFs, 1 round opened and cancelled ("What a run writes", the chapter's end).
- **Cross-cutting (Tasks 25–27):** one shared edit, the allow-list entry of EM2 (AS-R2). No test uploads to the import; no request is a `DELETE`; refusals use record ids that do not exist (EM-R6).

## Rulings of this plan

### The assembly's rulings (AS-R1–AS-R14)

- **AS-R1, the chapters' rulings apply, these on top.** The foundation's EF-R1–EF-R12 and the areas' TX-R1–TX-R13, EA-R1–EA-R12, ER-R1–ER-R9, ED-R1–ED-R9, EM-R1–EM-R12 (below, as drafted) bind their tasks. Where one of them and this list differ, this list wins and says why; where two chapters differ, the earlier chapter's ruling holds unless this list says otherwise.

- **AS-R2, the shared allow-list: four entries, in chain order.** Two chapters append to `e2e/support/allow-list.ts`: TX1 (`foreign-attachment-thumbnail`, `thumbnail-cancelled-on-leave`, TX-R9) and EM2 (`topbar-notifications-cancelled-by-redirect`, EM-R7). In plan order TX1 comes first (it replaces the file whole, from the foundation's one entry to three), and EM2's Step 4 then appends its entry: its exact old text, the last entry's `    reviewed: "2026-10-19",` / `  },` / `]`, occurs once on the chain too (only the last entry is followed by `]`), so the edit applies as printed and the list is the foundation's entry, TX1's two and EM2's one, in that order (the assembly's resolution, byte for byte). EM2's Step 5 eol line then prints `0 44` for `e2e/support/allow-list.ts`, not the area's `0 26`. The EF4 unit test caps the list at 5 entries (`ALLOW_LIST.length <= 5`); four is within it. FX8 changes `foreign-attachment-thumbnail` from status 500 to 404 (the route's answer after its fix). No other entry is added in this plan; a new one needs the same review (id, kind, reason, reviewed date).

- **AS-R3, EA3's allow-list pin.** EA3's unit test asserted `ALLOW_LIST.map((entry) => entry.id)` equals `["next-prefetch-cancelled"]` ("the reviewed allow-list is not widened for it"); with TX1 before it that is false. On the chain it asserts what it means: no entry lets the browser's offline error (`net::ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED`, "WebKit encountered an internal error") or its console line through. EA3's Step 1 block below is printed with this change (rehearsed on the chain: `6089b3e9`).

- **AS-R4, unit tests strip `\r`; the working tree may be CRLF.** Under `core.autocrlf=true` a checkout, a merge or a rebase writes every new text file with CRLF in the working tree (the assembly's cherry-picks did; so will Task 54's merge into `master`). Every unit test that reads a file strips `\r` before it compares lines (the foundation's and four areas' `read` did; TX1's did not and failed 7 tests on the chain). TX1's Step 1 block below is printed with `read` stripping `\r` (`782b20f2`). No test asserts a working-tree line ending.

- **AS-R5, counts.** The areas were rehearsed on the foundation alone (base 2318). Their Step 5 lines show the chain's totals first and the area rehearsal's in brackets (the table in Global Constraints). The +1 per task never changes.

- **AS-R6, the areas' helpers stay where they are.** Several helpers exist once per area: reading the asset form's masters and creating an asset through `POST /api/assets` (transactions, audit, repair, disposal, cross-cutting), Bangkok dates (transactions, repair), a toast settled before axe (`settle`, `settleToasts`/`recordSettled`, `expectToast`), a person change (`actAs`). Each area was replayed byte for byte from its text; moving them into `e2e/support/` now would void those rehearsals for no change in what is tested. Open issue 6 proposes the consolidation after the suite is green, as its own small plan.

- **AS-R7, one finding seen by several areas is fixed once and unmarked everywhere.** The suite found 27 app findings under area ids; several are one defect: the print `<dl>` (TX-A3, AR1, D1, M8), the QR name (TX-A2, AR2, M6), a scrolling region the keyboard cannot reach (TX-A8, D3, M9), the audit exports offered without permission (AU1, M1), the UTC default date (TX-N1, AU3). Each defect has one fix task (FX1–FX15) that, in the same commit, removes every `test.fail(…)` marker of it in every area (the test becomes the regression test, with a one-line comment saying which fix) and turns every unit pin that asserted the marker into its absence. The transactions area's `KNOWN_AXE` list (TX-R5) loses each entry with its fix and is empty after FX3. The "App findings" table near the end maps every area id to its task.

- **AS-R8, shared files.** Only the foundation edits `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `playwright.config.ts`, `eslint.config.mjs`, `.gitignore` and the fixtures (no area touched them: `git diff --stat 8bbf7217` of each area branch). The shared support files an area edits: `e2e/support/allow-list.ts` (TX1, EM2; AS-R2; FX8), `e2e/support/measure.ts` and `measure-core.ts` (EA3's offline window; SF1's load time). Nothing else outside an area's folder and its unit test changes in Tasks 6–27.

- **AS-R9, a finding that needs a decision is asked, never guessed** (spec §9). DX1–DX6 open with the question to the user (in Thai, with the options and a recommendation) and stop until the user answers in chat; an answer applies to that task only. Until then their tests stay expected to fail and the report (Task 52) lists them. A deferred decision is not a failure of Task 51.

- **AS-R10, one suite run at a time.** The controller runs one `playwright test` at a time on the database. The run-id collisions of the rehearsal (two agents in one minute after the day's `Z`) cannot happen then; `nextRunId` stays as EF2 wrote it (open issue 5).

- **AS-R11, the dev database must answer before a run.** Before every run (Tasks 0, 28, each Step 5, 51) the controller runs `node <tools>/select-e2e-counts.cjs` (SELECT only; it checks the `.env` names `asset_management_dev` and `asset_dev` and prints counts only). `query failed: ETIMEOUT` means the SQL Server does not answer (the assembly met it, DN2 met it): stop, tell the user, never start the suite then (its sign-in would stop at the database, which is safe, but nothing is learned).

- **AS-R12, the fix round's form.** Each fix task: **Step 1** appends its test to `tests/e2e-fixes.test.ts` (the source or pure-module test, plus the absence of the finding's markers); **Step 2** runs it red (rehearsed); **Step 3** the product edits, the marker edits and the pin edits, exactly as printed (an edits block for `node <atools>/edit.mjs`, or the Edit tool with the same old and new texts), and the message change file when there is one; **Step 4** the existing pins the change moves (listed; most fixes move none); **Step 5** the unit numbers (rehearsed) and the controller's build and E2E run of the finding's specs in all four projects (to be measured: the fixes were rehearsed at source level only); **Step 6** the commit. A fix that changes what a person sees or reads is listed for the user in Task 52 (the "Visible" column of the findings table).

- **AS-R13, the suite fix SF1.** `measure.recordPage` read the first document's navigation entry on every later client-side page, so the summary repeated one load time for pages reached by a click (SN2). SF1 records `null` when the page's path is not the entry's: load times stay recorded, never asserted (spec §8), and the report says "client navigation" for a `null`.

- **AS-R14, what this plan does not change.** No schema change but DX1's, and only on the user's yes (a manual migration, applied to `asset_management_dev` by the controller with the user's approval of the full SQL; Production at the deploy with its own approval, AGENTS.md); no write to `asset_management`; no change to the LDAP, settings, import, integration or company-system code; no new dependency beyond the spec's two; no CI; no deploy. The decisions the spec already made stay (the IT staff opener may not close a round: the code's rule, spec §6.2.4, "เลือก 3").

### The foundation (Tasks 1–5, EF1–EF5): its rulings

- **EF-R1, the version is the cache's.** Playwright 1.61.1 is the version whose `browsers.json` names `chromium-1228`, `chromium-headless-shell-1228` and `webkit-2311`, the builds in `%LOCALAPPDATA%/ms-playwright` (checked in npx caches: 1.58.2 is 1208/2248, 1.64 alphas are 1244+/2361+). Both packages are pinned exact (`-E`). Install with `PLAYWRIGHT_SKIP_BROWSER_DOWNLOAD=1`, `@playwright/test` first and `@axe-core/playwright` second: in one command npm placed `@axe-core/playwright`'s peer at the top as `playwright-core` 1.64.1 (browsers 1248), with 1.61.1 nested under `playwright`; two commands keep one `playwright-core`, 1.61.1, deduped. axe-core moves from 4.11.4 to 4.13.0 for `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` too (deduped; lint unchanged). `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 (2026-08-11) rather than 4.14.0 (2026-10-15, four days old).
- **EF-R2, the guard runs first, in the config.** Playwright starts `webServer` before `globalSetup` (`node_modules/playwright/lib/runner/index.js`, `createGlobalSetupTasks`: plugin setup, then global setups), so a guard only in the global setup would start the app on whatever `.env` names. `playwright.config.ts` calls `guardAppEnv(process.cwd())` when it loads: every `playwright test`, `--list` included, stops on anything but `asset_management_dev` / `asset_dev`. It reads the env files through `@next/env`'s `loadEnvConfig(root, false)` (what `next start` loads: `.env.production.local`, `.env.local`, `.env.production`, `.env`, the process environment winning) and puts `process.env` back (`resetEnv`), so no value reaches a worker through the read. The database is read as `src/lib/db-config.ts` reads it (`database=` of `DATABASE_URL`), the login is `DB_USER`. The global setup runs the same guard again and prints the names it checked. The approval settings come from `GET /api/admin/settings`, which fills a default the database does not hold in with its key as its id: the guard asserts the policy of all five and reports as stored only the rows with an id of their own.
- **EF-R3, the server is the suite's own.** `webServer` runs `next start -p <E2E_PORT>` with `reuseExistingServer: false`, so the server that answers is the one started after the guard, on the env the guard read. It sets `AUTH_URL` and `NEXTAUTH_URL` to the suite's base URL for that process (Auth.js builds its URLs from them; the process environment wins over `.env`). The build is the controller's (`npm run build`); a missing build stops `next start` with its own message.
- **EF-R4, the run id** is `E2E-` + month and day + the letter after the day's last run, found in the E2E department codes (`GET /api/departments?search=E2E-MMDD`) and the usernames (`GET /api/admin/users`); after Z, `T` and the time (`E2E-1019T1432`). The setup's first write is the department, so a run that stops before it leaves no trace and its letter is reused (the rehearsal's EF2 runs both took `G`, and EF3's setup then created `G`). Usernames are `e2e.<slug>-<suffix>` (`e2e.it-staff-1019a`, spec §7), role keys `e2e_<slug>_<suffix>` (the role key allows `[a-z0-9_]` only), display names `<run id> <role>`.
- **EF-R5, roles on the role form, the rest through the API.** There is no API that lists permissions (`/api/admin/roles` has POST only), so the four roles are created on `/th/admin/roles/new` by ticking the module row's action boxes (`span.tag` = the module key, the checkbox named by `adminRolesPage.actions`), saved with "บันทึกสิทธิ์", and read back on `/th/admin/roles/<id>/edit`: the ticked set must equal spec §7 exactly. The department, the location (a `Room`), the employees and the users go through their `POST` routes with the seed admin's session. The company is the first active company (by code) with an asset tag code and a branch; the branch its first by code. The employee route needs the `email` key present (`email: null`): the schema's `z.preprocess` takes a missing key as missing (the employee form sends `""`); not a bug of the form.
- **EF-R6, no sign-in is ever retried or guessed.** `signInOnce` waits until React has hydrated the login form (a submit before it would be the browser's own GET with the password in the URL), sets the password through the DOM (`locator.evaluate`, so no step title, trace or report holds it), checks the fields hold the account, submits once, and stops the run on a refusal ("the run stops and never tries again"). It also fails if any request carried `password=` in its URL. `retries: 0`; the setup project has `trace: "off"` and `screenshot: "off"`. The rehearsal's `scan-secrets.mjs` found none of the five passwords in the 63 report and result files.
- **EF-R7, users as spec §7, but the viewer is linked (finding A1).** The app cannot create a second user with no employee link: `users.employeeId` is a plain unique constraint (`users_employeeId_key`, no filter; `prisma/schema.prisma:973` `String? @unique`), SQL Server lets it hold one NULL, and the seed admin holds it, so the save answers 500 "Unexpected error". The viewer is linked to its own E2E employee until A1 is fixed (it still lands on the dashboard: `shouldUseEmployeeHome` is false with any overview permission), and the setup keeps an expected-to-fail test that creates an unlinked user; when A1 is fixed it passes, Playwright reports it, and the viewer goes back to unlinked.
- **EF-R8, the WebKit phone is a worker option.** `playwright.config.ts` keeps the four projects of the spec; `e2e/support/fixtures.ts` replaces the `browserName` fixture with `browserFor(e2eDevice, webkitOnPhone)` (worker scope) and the `userAgent` with the phone user agent of that browser (Pixel 7's for Chromium, iPhone 15's for WebKit, Playwright 1.61.1's descriptors). A spec file calls `runPhoneOnWebKit()` at its top level (`test.use({ webkitOnPhone: true })`). In the rehearsal the IT staff smoke ran WebKit on both phone projects and Chromium on both desktop projects.
- **EF-R9, the measurements.** An auto fixture (`measure`) starts with every test and checks after it: console `error` messages and page errors; responses ≥ 400 and failed requests, less the reviewed allow-list (`e2e/support/allow-list.ts`) and the refusals the test declares with `measure.expectRefusal` (a declared refusal that never happens fails too; the browser's own "Failed to load resource" line of a refused or allowed response goes with it); axe (`wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa`) on every page visited through `measure.visit` or recorded with `measure.recordPage`, failing on serious and critical, counting moderate and minor; the load and DOMContentLoaded times of the navigation (recorded, never asserted, spec §8) and `measure.timed` for steps that are not page loads; a full-page screenshot at the end (pass or fail) and Playwright's own on failure, with a trace kept on failure. The fixture attaches the test's row (`e2e-summary`) before it judges, so a failing test still has its row; `e2e/support/summary-reporter.ts` writes `e2e-results/summary.json` (tests, per-project counts with expected failures apart) and prints one line per project. Fixture callbacks are named `provide`: the React hooks lint reads a call of `use` as a hook (5 lint errors in the draft).
- **EF-R10, one allow-list entry.** `next-prefetch-cancelled`: a `?_rsc=` request that fails with `net::ERR_ABORTED` (WebKit: `cancelled`, `Load request cancelled`). Next prefetches the routes of the links on screen and cancels those it no longer needs; the first full run had 1–18 per dashboard visit and failed 11 of 16 smokes on them alone. A prefetch answered with an error status is still a failure. Reviewed 2026-10-19.
- **EF-R11, one worker, no retries, one setup per run.** `workers: 1`, `fullyParallel: false`: the workflows write through the app and some document numbers count the day's rows (the PM plan number has no retry, recon). The four projects share the run's setup (`dependencies: ["setup"]`); each workflow creates its own `E2E-` records per project (spec §11 "Run order").
- **EF-R12, the fixtures are binary and tiny.** `photo.png` (96×64 RGB, 396 bytes) and `evidence.pdf` (one A4 page reading "E2E evidence", deflated content stream, 623 bytes), each with a NUL byte so git never converts their line ends (a text PDF's xref offsets would break under `core.autocrlf`). They are written from base64 by `<tools>/b64.mjs` and checked by sha256.

### Asset transactions, the user's first worry (Tasks 6–12, TX1–TX7): its rulings

- **TX-R1, each test makes its own assets, per project.** A test creates the `E2E-` assets it changes, as IT staff, through `POST /api/assets` (the route the asset form posts to) with the form's whole body (the schema reads a missing optional key as missing, the foundation's N3; it holds for the asset and the checkout JSON too). Tags are `E2E-<run>-<project code>-<key>` (`E2E-1020B-PD-REG`: DL, DD, PL, PD for the four projects; the tag column holds 50), names `E2E-<run> <code> <Thai label>`, so the four projects never share an asset (spec §11 "Run order") and a person sees the marker in any list. The forms themselves are exercised where the workflow is the form: the asset form (TX1), the batch form (TX5), and every transaction form. The masters (the Notebook category, Ready, Good) are read from the new asset form's own options, since statuses and conditions have no API.
- **TX-R2, the second room is the area's.** Returns, transfers and bulk moves need a room to take the asset to. `ensureSecondLocation()` creates `E2E-<run>-L2` (`<run> ห้องปลายทาง E2E`, type Room, the run's branch) once per run as the seed admin (the global setup's saved session) through `POST /api/locations`, and finds it by its code on every later call (`GET /api/locations?search=`). No change to the foundation's setup (a shared file).
- **TX-R3, the desk delivery runs on Chromium, on the phone only.** Spec §8 asks for WebKit on the phone for the desk delivery, "the builder's call if WebKit fails for a reason outside the app". Playwright 1.61.1 has no touch drag (`Touchscreen` has `tap` only), and WebKit has no other way to send touch input; a synthetic `PointerEvent` of type touch cannot take pointer capture (`NotFoundError`, both browsers; `<tools-tx>/probe-touch.mjs`), which the pad calls. Chromium's own touch input (CDP `Input.dispatchTouchEvent`) reaches the pad as pointer events of type `touch`, as a finger does, and the test checks that type. So the desk delivery stays on Chromium (no `runPhoneOnWebKit()`), and runs only in the phone projects (`test.skip` on the desktop: there the handover is TX2's). Every other phone run of the area is Chromium as well (spec §8: Chromium for the rest). The WebKit phone stays covered by the foundation's IT staff smoke.
- **TX-R4, the tap Chromium's emulation loses.** After a touch drag sent through a second CDP session, Chromium drops the click of Playwright's next `tap()` (pointer and touch events arrive, the click does not); a plain page with no app code does the same (`<tools-tx>/probe-tap.mjs`: with or without pointer capture, the first tap after the stroke has 0 clicks, the second 1). `signOnPad` therefore taps the pad's title once after a finger stroke, so the person's next tap counts. Emulation, not the app: a real phone is a UAT item.
- **TX-R5, known findings.** An app finding that every visit of a page would trip (TX-A2 the asset page's QR code, TX-A3 the A4 documents, TX-A8 the phone's scrolling regions) is a `KnownAxe` entry in `known-findings.ts`: its rule and a pattern of its element. The area's page records drop exactly those violations (the counts stay in the summary; the test gets a "known app finding" annotation), so a workflow's other measurements still count; any other violation still fails. Each entry has its own expected-to-fail test in `findings.spec.ts`, which visits the page with the bare `measure` calls; when the app is fixed that test passes, Playwright reports it, and the fix task removes the `test.fail` line and the entry together. A finding inside one workflow (TX-A1, TX-A4–TX-A7) is an expected-to-fail test beside its workflow test in a serial describe, reading what the workflow test saw (a per-project `Map`), so the workflow runs once.
- **TX-R6, settle before axe.** axe read a success toast still fading in as low contrast (`color-contrast` on sonner's `div[data-title]`, the batch form on the phone, run `T2313`). `record()` waits until no finite animation runs (`document.getAnimations()`; a spinner's endless one does not count), then runs axe. `visit()` is `Measure.visit`'s two steps with the settle between them.
- **TX-R7, the double checkout as it happens.** The app hides an asset already handed over from a fresh handover form (it is not in the list) and disables the asset page's ส่งมอบ, so the refusal a person meets is a form opened before the first handover saved: two IT staff, or one form left open. TX2 opens a second page of the same session (`context.newPage()`) before the first saves, saves there first, then saves the stale form: `POST …/checkout` answers 409, declared with `measure.expectRefusal`; the asset stays with the first receiver; a fresh form no longer offers it. What the refusal says is TX-A1's own test.
- **TX-R8, the keyboard.** The phone with the keyboard open is the viewport at 390×360 (`PHONE_KEYBOARD_OPEN`, `openPhoneKeyboard`), the foundation's suggestion. A control is reachable when it scrolls into view and is the topmost element at its centre (`expectReachable`). TX1 checks the asset form's sticky save bar with the keyboard open, TX7 the handover form's save and the review dialog's confirm.
- **TX-R9, the register's thumbnails (shared edit, `e2e/support/allow-list.ts`).** The asset form and the bulk move send the person to the asset register, which shows the newest assets' thumbnails. An attachment row stores the absolute path of the upload folder that wrote it, so a row with a Production path (the dev database is a copy, wiki #15 #25 #43), or written by another checkout of the app (the rehearsal's parallel clones), answers `500 Invalid attachment path` from the thumbnail route; and the images still loading when the test moves on are cancelled. Two reviewed entries let exactly these through: `foreign-attachment-thumbnail` (a 500 of `/api/attachments/<uuid>/thumbnail`) and `thumbnail-cancelled-on-leave` (that URL failing with `net::ERR_ABORTED`/`cancelled`). A test's own photo is checked on its own asset page (`naturalWidth > 0`), never through these thumbnails. The foundation's EF4 test still passes (its records are other URLs); the allow-list has 3 entries (its limit is 5).
- **TX-R10, the register before moving on.** After a save that lands on `/th/assets`, the test waits for `networkidle` before its next page, so the register's own requests finish (the first draft cancelled a font and a thumbnail by leaving at once).
- **TX-R11, "prints" is the print button calling `window.print()`.** Headless Chromium has no print dialog; `expectPrints` replaces `window.print` with a counter on the document page, clicks the button (พิมพ์ใบส่งมอบ, พิมพ์ใบรับคืน, พิมพ์เอกสารโอนย้าย) and expects one call. The page itself is measured (axe, load time) and its number checked. A printed page on paper stays a UAT item.
- **TX-R12, dates in Bangkok.** A date the test types is `bangkokDate(days)` (UTC+7): the browser runs in `Asia/Bangkok` (the foundation's config).
- **TX-R13, the asset form's wrong entry is "no room".** The form fills the name from the chosen category while the name was never typed (`withSuggestedName`), so a form with no name typed saved an asset in the first draft (run `T2308`). The wrong entry a person makes is a required select left empty: the current room (ที่ตั้งปัจจุบัน). The test also waits for the tag's live duplicate check (its reason beside the save, `#asset-save-reason`) before pressing save, and checks that the wrong entry posted nothing.

### The field audit walk and its review, the user's second worry (Tasks 13–17, EA1–EA5): its rulings

- **EA-R1, one serial chain per project, one block per person.** `e2e/audit/field-audit.spec.ts` calls `runPhoneOnWebKit()` and `test.describe.configure({ mode: "serial" })` at its top level; each top-level `test.describe` holds one person's part with its own `asUser` (IT staff for blocks 1–4, the IT head for 5–6; block 7 holds one test of each). A failure skips the rest of the chain in that project (serial mode), never another project. The blocks share the project's records through `e2e/.state/<run>/audit-<project>.json` (written by block 1, the not-found finding id added by block 3), not through module variables: a worker may run two projects in a row (desktop light and dark share the worker options). In the phone projects the IT head works on the WebKit phone too (the browser is a per-file worker option; spec §6: "a person can use either").
- **EA-R2, the project's own records, through the app.** Per project (`e2e/audit/audit-plan.ts`, short codes DL, DD, PL, PD): a department (the round's scope, `<run>-<code>-D`, 20 characters at most), rooms A and B (`<run>-<code>-RA`, `-RB`), and eight assets `<run>-<code>-<suffix>` (room A: A1 matched, A2 mismatch, P1 the parent with C1 and C2 installed; room B: A4 not found, A5 offline; X1 of the run's own department in room A, outside the scope). The department and rooms go through `POST /api/departments` and `POST /api/locations` as the seed admin (IT staff hold no master-data permission; the foundation's setup creates the run's own the same way); the assets through `POST /api/assets` and the components through the install route `POST /api/assets/<parent>/components`, as IT staff. The category (Desktop Computer, Computer Component), status (พร้อมใช้งาน) and condition (ดี, พอใช้) ids come from the round form's own scope selects, the lists the person has on screen. The asset form itself is §6.1.1's (the transactions area); this area needs assets, not the form.
- **EA-R3, the round is the department's, opened at once.** Status "เปิดตรวจ", scope department = the project's; the preview lists the seven assets of the department (components included) and not X1; the save answers 201 with `generatedItems` 7 and the toast says so.
- **EA-R4, slow 4G on the phone, no CPU throttle.** Spec §8 asks for slow 4G and a CPU throttle on the audit walk; the walk's phone runs WebKit, which has no network or CPU emulation (CPU throttling is Chromium's CDP). The phone runs of blocks 2 and 3 delay every request of the page by a slow-4G round trip, 150 ms, through `page.route` (`scan.slowNetwork`; latency only, the bandwidth is the machine's). No CPU throttle anywhere; the desktop runs are not slowed. (Unsettled 2: a Chromium phone run of the walk would allow the CPU throttle.)
- **EA-R5, the offline window is the measurement's.** `context.setOffline(true)` makes the save fail, which is what the app's queue is for; meanwhile WebKit reports every failed request as "WebKit encountered an internal error" (`<atools>/offline-probe.cjs`; Chromium: `net::ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED`), too broad a text for the reviewed allow-list. So EA3 adds `measure.whileOffline(step)` (shared `e2e/support/measure.ts`, pure `splitOfflineRecords` in `measure-core.ts`): it sets the browser offline for `step` and back online after it, and only the records made inside that window with the browser's offline error (and their console lines) are the test's own, listed as allowed `offline-by-the-test`; anything else, and anything after the window, still fails. The allow-list stays the foundation's one entry.
- **EA-R6, a refusal the UI never sends is read from the page.** The review buttons and the close button are not offered to the refused person, so the route's own answer is read with a `fetch` from the page (`review.browserFetch`: the same session), after `measure.expectRefusal` declares it; the test asserts the exact status and body.
- **EA-R7, the opener's close is asserted on the IT head's own round.** IT staff are stopped by the permission (`audit:approve`) before the opener rule; the opener rule (`closeOwnRound`) needs an approver who opened the round. The IT head opens a round of room B through the round route (the body the form sends), marks A4 not found in it (their own finding: `reviewOwnFinding`), sees both refused (the note, the disabled close with the rule beside it, the routes' 403 bodies), and cancels the round with a reason, so no E2E round is left open.
- **EA-R8, the mismatch changes the department and the condition.** The user's case is "another department or branch"; the check asks for a photo only when the condition differs (`requiresCheckPhoto`, `src/lib/audit-scan-session.ts:275`), so the walk changes both, to reach the required photo (note N-AU1 for the user).
- **EA-R9, axe after the toasts settle.** A toast sliding in (sonner's 400 ms opacity transition) measured 4.04:1 contrast at 0 ms and passed at 1.5 s; `e2e/audit/settle.ts` waits until the toast region has no running transition before `measure.recordPage` wherever a toast may be on screen (note N-AU2).
- **EA-R10, the keyboard-open height.** On the phone, the matched check opens its note, the person taps it, and the viewport becomes 390×360 (what a phone keyboard leaves of the 390×664 screen): the note stays focused and on screen, the pinned save button wholly on screen; then back to 390×664 (spec §8).
- **EA-R11, the exports by permission are expected to fail.** No E2E role holds `audit:export` (spec §7's table), yet the findings page offers its Excel and PDF exports (and the round page its result and variance exports) to every audit viewer, and the download is the route's 403 body saved as `export.json` (finding AU1). Block 7 asserts the spec: IT staff are not offered the export (`test.fail`, AU1), and the findings export downloads an Excel file holding the round's findings (`test.fail`, U1: the user decides who holds `audit:export`). Playwright reports either when it passes.
- **EA-R12, typed search only.** The QR labels are not printed yet (the user, 2026-10-07): every asset is found by typing; the camera is not opened.

### Repair records and the PM plan (Tasks 18–21, ER1–ER4): its rulings

- **ER-R1, IT staff, all four projects, the phone on Chromium.** Spec §2 gives the repair record and the PM plan to IT staff on the computer; §6 runs every workflow four times; §8 keeps WebKit for the audit walk and the desk delivery, so these files do not call `runPhoneOnWebKit()`. In the rehearsal every page, button and dialog of the walk was reachable at 390×664: the record page's header actions ("ซ่อมเสร็จ", "ยกเลิกบันทึก", "พิมพ์") and the PM list's row buttons sit in the page on the phone too.
- **ER-R2, each test its own assets, through the app.** `createAsset` posts the asset as IT staff: Notebook, Ready, Good, the run's company, branch, department and room. The IT staff role has no category or status API (`/api/categories` needs `category:view`), so `readAssetMasters` reads the three ids from the selects of `/th/assets/new`, on a page of its own, not the test's measured page. The body names `purchaseDate`, `purchasePrice`, `warrantyStartDate`, `warrantyEndDate`, `licenseTotalSeats`, `licenseUsedSeats` (and the handover `expectedReturnDate`) as `null`: zod 4's `z.preprocess(…, ….optional())` reads a missing key as missing and answers `400 required` (the rehearsal's first run; the foundation's N3 for the employee API, the same cause). The arranging requests go through `page.request`, so they are not page events: the measurements judge the walk, not the arranging.
- **ER-R3, the in-use case through a permanent handover.** `handOverPermanently` posts the JSON handover (`permanent_assignment`, the run's `receiver1`, no due date): the asset is In Use with an active handover, as a staff member's own notebook is when it goes for repair. Repaired then brings it back In Use because the handover is active (`getRepairRestoreStatusName`), with the custodian kept.
- **ER-R4, each outcome three ways.** On the record page (the header's state badge, "สถานะปัจจุบัน", "ผู้ถือครอง", "ผลการซ่อม"), by `GET /api/assets/<id>` (the status `name`), and in the unit test against the app's own rule (`getRepairRecordCreateEffect`, `getRepairRecordCompleteEffect`, `getRepairRecordCancelEffect`, `calculateNextMaintenanceDueDate`).
- **ER-R5, the wrong entries (spec §6.7).** The repair workflow's first walk (ER1) saves with no problem (`กรุณาระบุข้อมูลนี้` under "อาการ / สิ่งที่ซ่อม", focus on it), then "ซ่อมเสร็จ" with the return date the day before the recorded date (`วันที่ซ่อมเสร็จต้องไม่ก่อนวันที่แจ้งซ่อม` under the date, focus on it: the client rule `notBefore`; the server has none); each is corrected and saved. The PM workflow (ER4) saves with no plan name first. ER2 and ER3 do not repeat it.
- **ER-R6, a finding is its own expected-to-fail test.** AR1 (the print), AR2 (the asset page), AR3 (the open search) each have a small test with `test.fail(true, "<id>: …")`, so the suite stays green and Playwright reports "expected to fail but passed" the day the finding is fixed (then the controller removes the `test.fail` line). The walks that pass those pages skip axe only there and keep console and request checks: ER2 opens the asset page with `page.goto` (not `measure.visit`), ER3 runs axe after the search closes; ER1 does not open the print.
- **ER-R7, the PM dates are fixed and past.** The plan is due 31 Jan 2026 and recorded done on 31 Jan 2026, whatever the run's day: overdue, so it shows in "PM ถึงกำหนด" (ordered by due date, 20 at most), and 31 Jan + 1 month is the month-end clamp (28 Feb 2026, not 3 Mar). The edit page's date input holds the exact next due date (`2026-02-28`; no API filters plans), and the PM list shows `กำหนด PM ครั้งถัดไป: 28/02/2569`. The PM list has no search and pages all plans, active first: the test opens it with `?pageSize=100`.
- **ER-R8, the area's files.** `e2e/repair/` holds the area's helpers and specs; `tests/e2e-repair.test.ts` is the area's own unit test file (not `tests/e2e-suite.test.ts`, so areas built side by side do not edit one file). Every helper that holds a step (`saveRepairForm`, `completeRecord`, `planAction`, `chooseAsset`) waits for the request it causes and checks its status, so a refused save fails at its step.
- **ER-R9, one worker, the run's order.** The four projects run one after another (the foundation's EF-R11) with their own assets; the record numbers (`MT-…`) and the plan numbers (`PM-…`) count the day's rows and are never reused (no delete), so a repeated run never collides on them.

### Disposal (Tasks 22–24, ED1–ED3): its rulings

- **ED-R1, one page follows the workflow; the person changes by session.** A disposal passes through three people (spec §2: IT staff request, the IT head approves, IT staff execute). Each spec file runs as IT staff (`asUser("itStaff")`); when the person changes, `actAs(page, "itHead" | "itStaff", e2eTheme)` clears the page's cookies and adds the user's cookies from the run's storage state plus the project's `theme` cookie, then the next `measure.visit` loads a fresh page. No sign-in happens (spec §11). The one `page` keeps the one `Measure`, so every step of every person is measured; the summary row's `user` is the file's user, and each page label names the person ("IT head: the pending request"). The foundation's reporter takes one summary per test, which is why a second browser context with its own `Measure` was not used.
- **ED-R2, each test's own room and assets.** Each test makes a room (`<run>-<DL|DD|PL|PD>-DSPn-R`, `locationType: "Room"`) through `POST /api/locations` with the seed admin's saved session (IT staff have no `location:create`; the setup creates the run's room the same way), and its assets (`<run>-<code>-DSPn-A1`…) through `POST /api/assets` as IT staff, Ready, with no holder, in that room. The category, the Ready status and the condition are read off the asset form's selects (`/th/assets/new`, a measured page) as the ids the form itself would send; IT staff have no `category:view`, and there is no status API. Why a room of its own: an audit round scoped to the run's room (spec §6.2's own walk) would hold these assets and block their disposal, and ED3's open round must reach nothing else. The asset form itself is §6.1's workflow; here the assets are the precondition.
- **ED-R3, arrangements through the API, as the person who would make them.** The IT head's own request (ED2) is posted as the IT head with the body the request form sends; ED3's round is posted as IT staff (`audit:create`) scoped to the room with `status: "open"`, and cancelled as the IT head (`PATCH /api/audit-rounds/<id>` `{ action: "cancel", reason }`, `audit:approve`). The round's own pages are spec §6.2's.
- **ED-R4, refusals.** A refusal the page itself receives is declared first with `measure.expectRefusal` (ED3: `POST /api/disposal-requests` 400, `DISPOSAL_ASSET_BLOCKED`). A refusal checked through `page.request` (IT staff's decision 403, the approver's execution 403 `DISPOSAL_SOD_CONFLICT`) is not page network, so it is asserted where it is made and not declared (a declared refusal that never comes fails the test). Segregation of duties that writes nothing is read through the bulk approval's `mode: "preview"`, which answers `blocked` / `DISPOSAL_SOD_CONFLICT` without a write (the seed admin in ED1, the IT head's own request in ED2).
- **ED-R5, findings in tests of their own, expected to fail.** A finding (D1, D2, D3) is not allowed to fail the workflow test it was found in: the workflow test stops short of it, and a test of its own in the same file (`test.describe.configure({ mode: "serial" })`, the state handed on in module variables) walks the step and is marked `test.fail(true, "finding Dn: …")`. An expected failure lets the next serial test run; when a finding is fixed, Playwright reports "expected to fail but passed" and the mark comes off with the fix. D3's test runs before D2's: it needs the request still approved, and D2's test, once D2 is fixed, executes it.
- **ED-R6, a toast is read by axe after its fade-in.** Sonner fades a toast in; axe run on a fading toast reads its colors at that opacity. The draft's ED3 failed `color-contrast` (serious) on the danger toast `div[data-title]` for this reason only: the same toast, once settled, passes in all four projects. `expectToast` waits until the toast has no running animation and its opacity is 1 before the test goes on.
- **ED-R7, the device.** Spec §2 puts disposal on the computer; spec §6 runs every workflow four times anyway. The phone runs on Chromium (no `runPhoneOnWebKit()`). On the phone the queue's row boxes show only after "เลือกหลายรายการ"; the detail pages render the next action twice (the header on the desktop, the action bar on the phone), so the specs click the one on screen (`visibleButton`).
- **ED-R8, bodies with every key the forms send.** The asset schema's preprocessed optional numbers and dates and the execution schema's preprocessed amounts answer `required` for a missing key (foundation note N3; the draft's first asset post was `400 {"fieldErrors":{"licenseTotalSeats":"required",…}}` and its first execution post 400). The API calls send those keys as `null`, as the forms do.
- **ED-R9, the disposal type and values.** Every request is `dispose` (the forms' default). Its execution needs a recipient and a reference document number (`getDisposalExecutionFieldErrors`) and evidence (`DISPOSAL_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED`); the final status is Disposed (the dialog's default). The evidence is the foundation's `e2e/fixtures/evidence.pdf`, on the request (ED1) or shared by the batch (ED2).

### Reports and exports, roles and permissions, errors under the field (Tasks 25–27, EM1–EM3): its rulings

- **EM-R1, the area's own folder and unit test.** `e2e/cross-cutting/` holds the three specs and their helpers; `tests/e2e-cross-cutting.test.ts` is this plan's alone (EM1 creates it, EM2 and EM3 append). The other areas' plans never touch them, and this plan touches one foundation file (`allow-list.ts`, one entry).
- **EM-R2, records through the app as the person who makes them.** IT staff register the assets through `POST /api/assets` with their session; the asset form's own choices (category, Ready and Draft statuses, the Good condition) are read from the form they open (`/th/assets/new`), since IT staff hold no `category:view` for the lookup APIs. A test whose person cannot register (the viewer, the employee) gets its assets from IT staff in a browser context of their own (`registerAssetsAsItStaff`), so the test's own page stays that person's. Handovers and returns that set a test up go through the checkout and checkin APIs; the forms themselves are walked in EM3. The asset API needs its preprocessed optional keys present (`null`; note N16, the foundation's N3).
- **EM-R3, every record carries the run and the project.** Tags are `<run id>-<project>-<slug>` (`E2E-1020P-phone-dark-FE-MT`), names `<run id>-<project> <Thai name>`, so the four projects never share a record and a person sees the marker in every list (spec §5, §11 "Run order"). Report pages are filtered with `?search=<run id>-<project>-<slug prefix>` so a view shows exactly the test's assets.
- **EM-R4, exports as spec §7 gives them.** `export-rights.ts` lists the six exports of the reports page with the permission each route requires; with spec §7's roles IT staff and the IT head may use the overview and the register, the viewer the register, and no E2E person a repair, disposal or findings export. The tests assert exactly that (shown or "ไม่มีสิทธิ์", downloads or 403), and the three module exports are proved to open with the seed admin's session (a GET of each, one project). Whether a person should hold them is the user's decision U1.
- **EM-R5, a download is a file that opens.** A click's download is saved under the test's output folder and opened with ExcelJS (every sheet's rows) or checked as a PDF (`%PDF-` … `%%EOF`); the register's rows are exactly the test's tags, the overview's "Total Assets" and "Total Purchase Value" are the test's.
- **EM-R6, refusals are pinned, and changed nothing.** `refusals.ts` lists the pages (with the permission `requirePagePermission` asks) and the requests (with the permission `requirePermission` asks); the unit test pins each against its file and checks that the permission comes before any `prisma.` read in that page or handler. Every request carries an empty body (or the one field the disposal PATCH picks its permission from) and a record id that does not exist (`NO_RECORD`), so even a route that failed to check could not change a record; no request is a `DELETE`. The approval inbox is not found (not a 403) for a person who may approve nothing.
- **EM-R7, refusals arrive as client navigations.** The dashboard's `loading.tsx` streams every page, so `redirect()` and `notFound()` reach the browser after the first load (the document answers 200): `visitRefused` waits for the access-denied page, then measures it; `expectNotFound` reads the not-found screen. The top bar's notifications fetch, cancelled by that navigation, is let through by one reviewed allow-list entry (`topbar-notifications-cancelled-by-redirect`; without it the viewer's refused-pages test failed with 9 problems, note N14). An error status on that fetch still fails.
- **EM-R8, the unfiltered register is never measured.** Its first page holds other runs' newest assets, whose photos may sit outside this server's upload folder and answer 500 (finding M7, note N10): the asset form is opened with `returnTo=/th/assets?search=<tag>` and the read-only check opens `/th/assets?search=<run>-<project>-ROLE2`.
- **EM-R9, one finding, one expected failure.** Each app finding has its own test with `test.fail(...)` and its reason, which fails in every project it names for that reason only (the run summaries show no other problem). A page that fails axe for a finding (the asset page M6, the documents M8, the return review on the phone M9) is measured only by that finding's test; the workflow tests that pass through it check what it shows and do not measure it, so each finding is counted once and each workflow test stays a clean pass.
- **EM-R10, no Playwright import in a spec.** The specs take `test`, `expect`, `asUser` from the foundation's fixtures; the helpers that need `Page`, `Locator`, `APIRequestContext`, `Browser` or `TestInfo` types live in `support/` (the foundation's EF5 test reads every spec for `from "@playwright/test"`).
- **EM-R11, an error under the field, as the V track defines it.** `expectFieldError` reads the error line `#<id>-error` with the app's sentence, the control marked invalid (`aria-invalid` on an input, `data-invalid` on a SearchableSelect's button; a radio group's fieldset carries neither), named by the control's `aria-describedby`, and focus on the first wrong field (the group's first radio for the handover mode). After the correction, before the save, `expectNoFieldError` sees it gone (V track spec §4.3). The asset form's save waits for its live duplicate check (note N17), so the duplicate test waits for the live error first.
- **EM-R12, saves that stay inside the test's own records.** The audit round's scope is the run's room and the Draft status, and its asset is the test's own Draft asset, so the round never holds another area's asset (a draft round would block their disposal). The bulk move is not walked here: the run has one room, and moving E2E assets into a real room would put them in that room's future audits (handed on). The review dialogs' decisions (findings, disposal) belong to the audit and disposal areas.

## Review Focus

Seven failure modes that would bite the user. Each is held by the tests or the steps named.

1. **The suite writes to the wrong database, or to a company system.** The guard reads the env `next start` reads (`@next/env`) and stops on anything but `asset_management_dev` / `asset_dev`, before the web server starts (EF-R2, EF2's test and its three live refusals); AS-R11's SELECT probe before every run; no LDAP, settings save, import or deletion anywhere (EF2's and EM2's pins; EM-R6's empty bodies and missing record ids).
2. **A failed sign-in reaches the company AD.** `signInOnce` signs each known account once, never retries, stops the run on a refusal (EF-R6, EF2's test); `retries: 0`; passwords set through the DOM, never in a trace; `scan-secrets.mjs` after every run.
3. **A workflow passes while the business rule it walks is broken.** Each area asserts the state after each step three ways where it can (the page, a GET of the record, the app's own rule in the unit test: ER-R4), the refusals with their exact status and reason (`measure.expectRefusal`, a declared refusal that never comes fails), and segregation of duties as the code has it (EA-R6, EA-R7, ED-R4).
4. **A measurement hides a failure.** The allow-list holds four reviewed entries (AS-R2); `known-findings.ts` drops exactly a rule on its element and is emptied by FX1–FX3 (AS-R7); every expected failure fails for its own reason only (the summaries' problem lists); SF1 stops repeating a load time.
5. **A fix breaks a workflow it does not name.** Every fix runs the whole unit suite (Step 5) and the controller's Task 51 runs every spec in all four projects after the last fix; FX1 (every print), FX3 (every dialog body), FX8 (every thumbnail) and FX12 (the cancel dialog) are shared components: their Step 5 runs every spec that opens them.
6. **A decision is guessed.** DX1–DX6 stop at their question (AS-R9); the report lists what is left with the user.
7. **The phone runs prove less than they seem.** Emulation is not a phone: the desk delivery runs on Chromium (TX-R3), the walk's WebKit phone has no CPU throttle (EA-R4), Chromium's emulated tap after a finger stroke needs a second tap (TX-R4). Task 52's UAT checklist sends each to a person on a real phone.

### The foundation (Tasks 1–5, EF1–EF5): its interfaces

- `import { test, expect, asUser, runPhoneOnWebKit } from "../support/fixtures.ts"` in every spec; never `@playwright/test`.
- `asUser("itStaff" | "itHead" | "viewer" | "employee")` at the top of a spec file; `runPhoneOnWebKit()` for the audit walk and the desk delivery.
- Fixtures: `measure` (`visit(path, label)`, `recordPage(label)`, `timed(label, fn)`, `expectRefusal({ method?, url: RegExp, status, reason })`, `screenshot(name)`), `e2eTheme`, `e2eDevice`, `browserName`.
- `readRunState()` → `{ runId, baseURL, database, login, approval, startedAt }`; `readRunData()` → `{ companyId, branchId, departmentId, locationId, employees: { itStaff, itHead, viewer, employee, receiver1, receiver2 }, users }` (`e2e/support/state.ts`). Every new record of a workflow carries `runId` (and the project name where desktop and phone must not share it).
- `e2e/fixtures/photo.png`, `e2e/fixtures/evidence.pdf`.
- Pure helpers for unit tests: `e2e/support/{matrix,paths,env-guard,run-plan,measure-core,allow-list}.ts`.

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** Create `tests/e2e-suite.test.ts` (LF) with exactly:

```ts
import assert from "node:assert/strict"
import { existsSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync } from "node:fs"
import path from "node:path"
import test from "node:test"
import { browserFor, E2E_PROJECTS, phoneUserAgent, PHONE_USER_AGENTS } from "../e2e/support/matrix.ts"

// The E2E workflow suite's foundation (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-e2e-workflow-suite-design.md §5, §7, §8;
// the foundation tasks EF1–EF5). One test per task, appended in order. The suite itself runs with `npm run test:e2e`
// against a running app; these pin what can be checked without one. The working tree is CRLF on Windows: every read
// strips \r.
const read = (file: string) => readFileSync(file, "utf8").replace(/\r/g, "")

function filesUnder(directory: string): string[] {
  if (!existsSync(directory)) return []
  return readdirSync(directory).flatMap((entry) => {
    const full = path.join(directory, entry)
    return statSync(full).isDirectory() ? filesUnder(full) : [full.replaceAll("\\", "/")]
  })
}

test("the E2E suite is its own Playwright project: four device × theme runs, the browser cache's version, out of npm test (e2e foundation task 1, EF1)", () => {
  // Spec §6: every workflow runs four times; §8: the desktop 1440×900, the phone 390×664 with touch, DPR 3 and a mobile
  // user agent.
  assert.deepEqual(E2E_PROJECTS.map((project) => project.name), ["desktop-light", "desktop-dark", "phone-light", "phone-dark"])
  for (const project of E2E_PROJECTS) {
    assert.equal(project.theme, project.name.endsWith("dark") ? "dark" : "light", project.name)
    if (project.device === "desktop") {
      assert.deepEqual([project.viewport, project.deviceScaleFactor, project.isMobile, project.hasTouch], [{ width: 1440, height: 900 }, 1, false, false], project.name)
    } else {
      assert.deepEqual([project.viewport, project.deviceScaleFactor, project.isMobile, project.hasTouch], [{ width: 390, height: 664 }, 3, true, true], project.name)
    }
  }
  // Spec §8: WebKit on the phone only for a file that asks (the audit walk, the desk delivery); the desktop on Chromium.
  assert.equal(browserFor("phone", true), "webkit")
  assert.equal(browserFor("phone", false), "chromium")
  assert.equal(browserFor("desktop", true), "chromium")
  assert.equal(phoneUserAgent("desktop", "chromium"), undefined)
  assert.match(phoneUserAgent("phone", "webkit") ?? "", /iPhone.*Mobile.*Safari/)
  assert.match(PHONE_USER_AGENTS.chromium, /Android.*Mobile Safari/)

  // The config builds its projects from the matrix, finds only *.spec.ts and the setup file, never retries.
  const config = read("playwright.config.ts")
  assert.match(config, /testDir: "e2e"/)
  assert.match(config, /\.\.\.E2E_PROJECTS\.map\(\(project\) => \(\{/)
  assert.match(config, /testMatch: \/\\\.spec\\\.ts\$\//)
  assert.match(config, /retries: 0,/)

  // The pinned versions; Playwright 1.61.1's browsers are the builds in the machine's cache (chromium-1228,
  // chromium_headless_shell-1228, webkit-2311), so nothing is downloaded.
  const pkg = JSON.parse(read("package.json"))
  assert.equal(pkg.scripts["test:e2e"], "playwright test")
  assert.equal(pkg.devDependencies["@playwright/test"], "1.61.1")
  assert.equal(pkg.devDependencies["@axe-core/playwright"], "4.13.0")
  const browsers = JSON.parse(read("node_modules/playwright-core/browsers.json")).browsers as { name: string; revision: string }[]
  const revision = (name: string) => browsers.find((browser) => browser.name === name)?.revision
  assert.deepEqual([revision("chromium"), revision("chromium-headless-shell"), revision("webkit")], ["1228", "1228", "2311"])

  // npm test sweeps tests/ for *.test.*; the suite's files are *.spec.ts and *.setup.ts under e2e/, never *.test.*.
  const runner = read("scripts/run-tests.mjs")
  assert.match(runner, /const testFiles = collectTestFiles\("tests"\)/)
  assert.equal((runner.match(/collectTestFiles\("/g) ?? []).length, 1, "run-tests.mjs collects from one folder")
  assert.deepEqual(filesUnder("e2e").filter((file) => /\.test\.(?:mjs|js|ts)$/.test(file)), [])

  // The report, the artifacts, the run state and the seed sign-in are git-ignored.
  const ignored = read(".gitignore").split("\n").map((line) => line.trim())
  for (const entry of ["/e2e-report/", "/e2e-results/", "/e2e/.state/", ".env*"]) assert.ok(ignored.includes(entry), `.gitignore has ${entry}`)
})
```

# run summary, and the run state (run id, users with their generated passwords, sessions). .env.e2e, the seed

  - Create `e2e/support/matrix.ts` (LF) with exactly:

```ts
// The E2E suite's device × theme matrix (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-e2e-workflow-suite-design.md §6, §8).
// Plain data and pure functions: playwright.config.ts builds its projects from it, and tests/e2e-suite.test.ts
// (node --test) imports it, so it imports nothing.

export type E2eDevice = "desktop" | "phone"
export type E2eTheme = "light" | "dark"
export type E2eBrowser = "chromium" | "webkit"

// The options a project sets (playwright.config.ts) and the suite's fixtures read (e2e/support/fixtures.ts). The
// browser is a worker option: a spec file asks for WebKit on the phone with runPhoneOnWebKit().
export type E2eTestOptions = { e2eTheme: E2eTheme }
export type E2eWorkerOptions = { e2eDevice: E2eDevice; webkitOnPhone: boolean }

export type E2eProject = {
  name: string
  device: E2eDevice
  theme: E2eTheme
  viewport: { width: number; height: number }
  screen: { width: number; height: number }
  deviceScaleFactor: number
  isMobile: boolean
  hasTouch: boolean
}

// Spec §8: the desktop is 1440×900; the phone is 390×664, the visible area with the browser bars, with touch, DPR 3
// and a mobile user agent.
export const DESKTOP = {
  viewport: { width: 1440, height: 900 },
  screen: { width: 1440, height: 900 },
  deviceScaleFactor: 1,
  isMobile: false,
  hasTouch: false,
}
export const PHONE = {
  viewport: { width: 390, height: 664 },
  screen: { width: 390, height: 844 },
  deviceScaleFactor: 3,
  isMobile: true,
  hasTouch: true,
}

// The phone's user agent follows the browser that runs it (Playwright 1.61.1's Pixel 7 and iPhone 15 descriptors).
export const PHONE_USER_AGENTS: Record<E2eBrowser, string> = {
  chromium: "Mozilla/5.0 (Linux; Android 14; Pixel 7) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/149.0.7827.55 Mobile Safari/537.36",
  webkit: "Mozilla/5.0 (iPhone; CPU iPhone OS 17_5 like Mac OS X) AppleWebKit/605.1.15 (KHTML, like Gecko) Version/26.5 Mobile/15E148 Safari/604.1",
}

// Every workflow runs four times (spec §6): desktop light, desktop dark, phone light, phone dark.
export const E2E_PROJECTS: readonly E2eProject[] = [
  { name: "desktop-light", device: "desktop", theme: "light", ...DESKTOP },
  { name: "desktop-dark", device: "desktop", theme: "dark", ...DESKTOP },
  { name: "phone-light", device: "phone", theme: "light", ...PHONE },
  { name: "phone-dark", device: "phone", theme: "dark", ...PHONE },
]

// Spec §8: the phone runs on WebKit for the field audit walk and the desk delivery only, on Chromium for the rest; the
// desktop always runs on Chromium. A spec file opts in with runPhoneOnWebKit() (e2e/support/fixtures.ts).
export function browserFor(device: E2eDevice, webkitOnPhone: boolean): E2eBrowser {
  return device === "phone" && webkitOnPhone ? "webkit" : "chromium"
}

export function phoneUserAgent(device: E2eDevice, browser: E2eBrowser): string | undefined {
  return device === "phone" ? PHONE_USER_AGENTS[browser] : undefined
}

// The project a test runs in, read back from its name.
export function projectNamed(name: string): E2eProject | undefined {
  return E2E_PROJECTS.find((project) => project.name === name)
}
```

  - Create `e2e/support/paths.ts` (LF) with exactly:

```ts
// Where the E2E suite keeps what it writes. Every path is relative to the repo root, the working directory of
// `npm run test:e2e`, and every one of them is git-ignored (.gitignore, "E2E suite"). Imports nothing but node:path, so
// tests/e2e-suite.test.ts can load it.
import path from "node:path"

// The app under test: a production build served by next start on its own port (spec §5, "The app under test").
export const DEFAULT_E2E_PORT = 3100
export function e2ePort(env: Record<string, string | undefined> = process.env): number {
  const port = Number(env.E2E_PORT ?? DEFAULT_E2E_PORT)
  if (!Number.isInteger(port) || port < 1024 || port > 65535) throw new Error(`E2E_PORT must be a port number from 1024 to 65535`)
  return port
}
export function e2eBaseUrl(env: Record<string, string | undefined> = process.env): string {
  return `http://localhost:${e2ePort(env)}`
}

// Playwright's HTML report, its per-test artifacts (traces, screenshots) and the run summary.
export const REPORT_DIR = "e2e-report"
export const RESULTS_DIR = "e2e-results"
export const ARTIFACTS_DIR = `${RESULTS_DIR}/artifacts`
export const SUMMARY_FILE = `${RESULTS_DIR}/summary.json`

// The run's state: the run id (run.json, rewritten by every run), and per run what its setup created (run-data.json,
// with the users' generated passwords) and its browser sessions. Never printed, never committed.
export const STATE_DIR = "e2e/.state"
export const RUN_FILE = `${STATE_DIR}/run.json`
export const ADMIN_STATE_FILE = `${STATE_DIR}/seed-admin.json`
export function runDir(runId: string): string {
  return `${STATE_DIR}/${runId}`
}
export function runDataFile(runId: string): string {
  return `${runDir(runId)}/run-data.json`
}
export function authStateFile(runId: string, userKey: string): string {
  return `${runDir(runId)}/auth/${userKey}.json`
}

// The seed admin's sign-in, written by the controller (never committed: .env* is ignored).
export const SEED_LOGIN_FILE = ".env.e2e"

export function fromRoot(relative: string, root: string = process.cwd()): string {
  return path.resolve(root, relative)
}
```

// Kept out of `npm test`: its files are e2e/**/*.spec.ts and e2e/setup/*.setup.ts, never *.test.ts under tests/.
import { defineConfig } from "@playwright/test"
import { E2E_PROJECTS, type E2eTestOptions, type E2eWorkerOptions } from "./e2e/support/matrix.ts"
import { ARTIFACTS_DIR, e2eBaseUrl, REPORT_DIR } from "./e2e/support/paths.ts"

const baseURL = e2eBaseUrl()

export default defineConfig<E2eTestOptions, E2eWorkerOptions>({
  testDir: "e2e",
  outputDir: ARTIFACTS_DIR,
  // The workflows write to the dev database through the app; one worker keeps the run in a readable order.
  workers: 1,
  fullyParallel: false,
  // A retry would sign in again and create a second set of records: a failure is reported, never retried.
  retries: 0,
  forbidOnly: true,
  timeout: 120_000,
  expect: { timeout: 10_000 },
  reporter: [["list"], ["html", { outputFolder: REPORT_DIR, open: "never" }]],
  use: {
    baseURL,
    locale: "th-TH",
    timezoneId: "Asia/Bangkok",
    actionTimeout: 15_000,
    navigationTimeout: 30_000,
  },
  projects: [
    // Every workflow four times (spec §6): desktop and phone, light and dark.
    ...E2E_PROJECTS.map((project) => ({
      name: project.name,
      testMatch: /\.spec\.ts$/,
      use: {
        viewport: project.viewport,
        screen: project.screen,
        deviceScaleFactor: project.deviceScaleFactor,
        isMobile: project.isMobile,
        hasTouch: project.hasTouch,
        e2eDevice: project.device,
        e2eTheme: project.theme,
      },
    })),
  ],
})
